It sits OUTSIDE the seven-domain split, as a **leaf**: it is never held while acquiring a domain mutex, and no domain mutex is held across `enqueueSend`. `dispatchOutboundAnnouncePlaneFrameWithCaps` captures the session under `peerMu.RLock` and enqueues only after releasing it; `enqueuePeerSendItem` resolves the session (`activePeerSession`) and the peer state (`peerState`) through their own short lock sections and enqueues afterwards. The fence therefore adds no edge to the canonical order.

Why a fence and not `close(sendCh)`: producers run on arbitrary goroutines and must neither block nor panic, and a producer racing a `close` panics on send. Flipping the flag under the same mutex the producers hold during their non-blocking send waits out any in-flight producer and refuses every later one, so the drain that follows provably sees the whole residue.

The SAME rule holds one layer down, and for the same reason: `NetCore.sendCh` is never closed either. It cannot use a mutex fence, because some of its producers (`queueFrameBlocking`, behind `SendRawSyncBlocking` and `SendRawBlockingCtx`) wait inside the offer for a slot that only the writer can free — a fence would make `Close` wait for a producer that is waiting for `Close`. It uses a monotonic atomic gate instead, read by every producer on both sides of its offer, and `NetCore.Close` raises that gate BEFORE it touches the socket or signals the writer. The writer's exit signal is a separate `closing` channel, so the queue itself is never closed under a live sender. A frame a racing producer still deposits is answered by the gate and released with the buffer when the `NetCore` is collected. The gate is also what NAMES the teardown to a producer already past the door: both the socket failure and the orderly `Close` wake it on the same `writerDone` channel, so the reason is read off the gate (`teardownStatus`) instead of being inferred from the wakeup — otherwise a caller's own `Close` came back as `SendWriterDone`, which is the answer that invites another `Close`. See `docs/protocol/network_core.md`, "Queue ownership".

**There is no terminal callback to keep out of the locks.** The outbound contract is data, not a hook: the writer reads `SendUntil` and `WriteGrace` off the ticket and nothing calls back up. `enqueueSend` still splits the guarded offer into `offerSend` (no `*Locked` suffix — it takes `ps.sendMu` itself), so the mutex is held for the offer and for nothing else, and `closeSendQueue` still drains after releasing `sendMu`. Refusal is reported to the producer as `false` rather than through a callback, because the producer owns the fallback policy (pending ring, inbound fallback).

Who drains what, per death path (every path must cover BOTH queues):

| Path | Upper queue (`sendCh`) | Lower queue (`NetCore.sendCh`) |
|---|---|---|
| socket write error / write-grace expiry | serve loop exits → `defer closeSendQueue` | writer shuts the gate (`gateSocketFailed`), drains what is buffered, then stays in drain-only mode |
| `servePeerSession` exit (ctx cancel, peer error, writer death, heartbeat stall, panic through `crashlog.DeferRecover`) | `defer closeSendQueue` | `peerSession.Close` → `NetCore.Close` |
| `peerSession.Close` before the serve loop ever runs (handshake failure, CM generation mismatch) | `closeSendQueue` first, then the transport | `NetCore.Close` shuts the gate and signals `closing`; the writer drains the queue before `Close` returns |
| `NetCore.Close` | — | gate shut first (producers get `SendChanClosed`), then the socket, then `closing`, then wait on `writerExited` (NOT `writerDone`: after a write failure the writer still owns `sendCh`) |
| queue refusal (no session, stalled peer, saturated queue, closed channel, marshal error) | `enqueueSend` / `enqueuePeerSendItem` answer `false` at the door, so the frame provably never started a write | `SendRawTracked` answers a non-`SendOK` status. Refusals AT THE DOOR (`SendBufferFull`, `SendMarshalError`, the gate read before the offer) prove the frame never entered the queue; a refusal read AFTER the offer proves only that the queue will not write it — see `docs/protocol/network_core.md`, "Tracked sends" |

An expired `SendUntil` is deliberately absent from that table: it is not a queue death at all. The writer skips the frame immediately before `rawConn.Write`, the connection stays up, and the frames behind it keep going.

`markPeerDisconnected` deliberately appears nowhere in that table either: it only mutates health/state and owns no queue. Every path that reaches it also reaches either the serve-loop exit or `peerSession.Close`, which are the two owners of the fence.

**Ordering contract — the fence is raised BEFORE the peer is published as gone.** A producer reads two facts as one decision: the peer-state gate (`peerAcceptsOutboundFrames`, written by `markPeerDisconnected`) and the queue's own answer. Only this order makes the pair honest. Published first, the disconnect leaves a window in which a producer admitted a moment earlier still deposits its frame into a queue the same teardown is about to discard — and the datagram emitter, which treats "the queue accepted it" as a delivery, ENDS its walk over the peer's remaining connections there, so the frame reaches neither this socket nor the next candidate. The two teardowns that own a queue implement it: `retirePeerSession` (`closeSendQueue` → `markPeerDisconnected`) on the dialled tier and `trackInboundDisconnect` (`NetCore.ShutSendQueue` → `markPeerDisconnected`) on the accepted one. Both fence UNCONDITIONALLY — the accepted tier does so even when this was not the last reference holding the health row up — and both do it outside every mutex, so the pair adds no edge to the canonical lock order.

The contract is ENFORCED rather than documented. `send_admission_race_test.go` interleaves one producer and one teardown through two seams that are nil in production: `Service.sendAdmissionBarrier` parks the PRODUCER inside the admission window (past the peer-state gate, before the queue offer), and `Service.peerTeardownBarrier` parks the TEARDOWN, on a goroutine of its own, BETWEEN its two publications. Both halves are needed and neither substitutes for the other: without the producer half the reversed order is simply refused by the gate and looks correct; without the teardown half the teardown runs to completion inside the producer's barrier and the resumed producer sees the same world whichever way round the two statements are written — which is exactly how an earlier version of these tests stayed green on the reversed implementation. Swapping the two publications on either tier turns the corresponding test red.

#### `gossipMu` — the local-change subscriber fence

`s.events` (gossip domain) is a set of subscriber inboxes, and the cancel returned by `SubscribeLocalChanges` CLOSES the inbox it registered. That makes the publisher's lock discipline part of the channel's safety, not just of the map's: `emitLocalChange` offers the event to every inbox while holding `gossipMu.RLock`, so the closer — which takes `gossipMu.Lock` — waits out every publisher already inside an offer and refuses every later one, because by then the map entry is gone. Snapshotting the set under the lock and offering after releasing it is NOT equivalent: the snapshot names channels the cancel is free to close in the gap, and a send on a closed channel is a panic in whichever goroutine happened to emit the event.

Holding a domain mutex over the offers does not breach the "no I/O under a lock" rule: every offer is non-blocking (`select` with `default`), so the section is bounded by the number of subscribers and cannot re-enter `Service`. The side effect — the warn line for a full inbox — is emitted by `emitLocalChange` AFTER `offerLocalChangeToSubscribers` has released the lock, per the cross-domain rule above.

### Reader path invariants

RPC hot paths that already read atomic snapshots (`fetch_network_stats`, `fetch_peer_health`, `get_peers`) remain lock-free; Phase 2 does not change them. What Phase 2 changes is the *refresher* goroutine's exposure to unrelated-domain contention.

Per-snapshot refresher lock footprint today:

- `networkStatsSnapshot` / `peerHealthSnapshot` — short `peerMu.RLock` (plus `deliveryMu.RLock` nested where per-peer queued-count is rendered). No IP-state callbacks, no `statusMu` hold.
- `cmSlotsSnapshot` — `cm.mu.RLock` only (separate mutex inside `ConnectionManager`, not covered by this split).
- `peersExchangeSnapshot` — more complex. `rebuildPeersExchangeSnapshot` takes a short `peerMu.RLock` for `persistedMeta` / `health`, releases it, then calls `peerProvider.Candidates()`. That Candidates call fires callbacks back into the Service: `BannedIPsFn` acquires `ipStateMu.RLock`, `RemoteBannedFn` acquires `peerMu.RLock → ipStateMu.RLock` in the canonical order, and `ConnectedFn` acquires `peerMu.RLock → deliveryMu.RLock`. A burst of IP-state writers (ban updates, observed-IP convergence) or of delivery writers can therefore still delay this particular rebuild.
- `routingSnapshot` — `routing.Table.t.mu.Lock` (exclusive: `SnapshotIncremental` consumes the per-identity snap dirty-set and updates the reuse cache; separate mutex inside the `routing` package, not covered by this split). The projection build is gated by `routing.Table.ConsumeDirty()` (CAS true→false) so a steady-state idle node skips the rebuild entirely; the publisher rebuilds only when some writer flagged the table dirty since the last consume. The binding rule is "any change to a published snapshot field (`Routes`, `FlapState`, `Health`, `CapStats`) sets the coarse dirty flag"; the writers fall into two non-exhaustive groups — *route-projection writers* that change `Routes` and so also mark the per-identity incremental dirty-set (`UpdateRoute`, `WithdrawRoute` / `InvalidateUplinkClaim`, `AddDirectPeer`, `ConfirmHopAck` promotion; `RemoveDirectPeer`, `InvalidateTransitRoutes`, `TickTTL` expiry force a full re-copy), and *snapshot-field-only writers* that change `FlapState` / `Health` / `CapStats` but not the routes, so the incremental build still reuses every route slice (`MarkHopAck`, `MarkProbeAck` / `MarkProbeFailure`, `MarkHopFailure`, `TickHealth`, black-hole cooldown clear, `RecordSuccessfulRouteAdd`, and the SeqNo flap-cap engage on armed `AnnounceTo` / `AnnounceTargetFor`). The publisher derives reachability and the presence `hasTransit` classification together in one O(routes+health) pass with one Dead-health index and one classification map; the lock-free identity-only cache is a compact immutable slice, not a second full-size map. Bulk reachability readers clone or iterate that slice instead of calling `BestRoute` per identity. Cached consumers (`Service.RoutingSnapshot()` for RPC, `Service.reachableIDsFrame()` for desktop remote-RPC, the file router's `RouteSnap` callback used by transit forwarding inside `HandleInbound`) take no routing-table mutex on the read path. If a witnessed final-route transition updates a trusted contact's `last_online_at`, the refresher queues `trustStore.recordLastOnlineAt` through the tracked `Service.goBackground` runner after publishing the snapshot. The JSON marshal/write/rename therefore performs no filesystem I/O on the refresher goroutine; `WaitBackground` joins it during shutdown and tests. `routeTableHandler` additionally enriches each unique next-hop identity through `RoutingProvider.PeerTransport`, which currently takes `peerMu.RLock` — that handler therefore stays exposed to peer-domain writer storms even after the routing-table coupling was eliminated; migrating `PeerTransport` onto a cached peer-transport view is tracked separately. Three production callsites deliberately bypass the cache and read the routing table directly via `routing.Table.Lookup(peer)` — a per-destination read that takes `t.mu.RLock` for an O(K) slice scan over only that destination's routes, not for the whole table: `file_integration.isPeerReachable` (gates user-initiated file transfers), `filerouter.Router.SendFileCommand` (locally-originated send path, wired through `RouterConfig.RouteLookup` to the same Lookup), and `filerouter.Router.ExplainRoute` (diagnostic surface, must mirror the live send). All three need immediate visibility of a route accepted seconds before the call — an ~2–2.5 s-stale view (`routingSnapshotMinInterval`'s 2 s floor plus the next refresh tick) would surface as "isPeerReachable says yes, SendFile fails" or as "ExplainRoute shows a path the live send cannot use". `isPeerReachable` holds `s.peerMu.RLock` only for the peer-domain probe and releases it before the routing read, so the two locks remain sequential, not nested; the file-router callsites take no Service-domain mutex at all on the locally-originated path. The transit forwarding path inside `HandleInbound` keeps using the cached `RouteSnap` because in-flight frames carry their own metadata and an ~2–2.5 s delay (the routing snapshot's coalescing floor plus a refresh tick) on a freshly added route is harmless there. Since the copy-on-write incremental snapshot landed, the publisher calls `routing.Table.SnapshotIncremental` (which takes `t.mu.Lock`, not `RLock`, for the brief projection build: it consumes a per-identity dirty-set the mutation wrappers maintain and reuses the unchanged buckets of the previous snapshot, so the per-rebuild allocation is proportional to the churned identities rather than the whole table). The exclusive hold is bounded — at most once per `routingSnapshotMinInterval` and shorter than the old full deep copy — and stays inside the routing package's own mutex, so the canonical Service lock order is unaffected. The single-threaded publisher contract is load-bearing here: `SnapshotIncremental` caches the previous projection and consumes the dirty-set, so no second concurrent caller may use it — any other consumer that needs an always-complete copy calls `routing.Table.Snapshot` (still `RLock`, still a full deep copy). Bulk mutations (`RemoveDirectPeer`, `InvalidateTransitRoutes`, `TickTTL`) force a full re-copy, and the publisher additionally upgrades a rebuild that is happening anyway (the table was dirty) to a full re-copy once `routingSnapshotFullInterval` of wall-clock time has elapsed since the last full one, as a self-heal net for any mutation site that failed to mark its identity dirty. This never wakes a clean table — a missed mark can only stale the published snapshot if a rebuild ran at all (coarse `dirty` set, per-identity mark missed), so the heal only rides along on a future dirty rebuild and the idle-skip invariant above is preserved. The wall-clock cadence (driven by the `atomic.Int64 Service.lastRoutingFullSnapAtNanos` timestamp, set on the single snapshot refresher plus the sequential startup prime) is deliberate: a pass counter would drift with the refresher tick rate because idle clean-skips do not advance `lastRoutingSnapAtNanos`, so the throttle lets every 500 ms tick through and a counter would fire the full roughly twice as often as intended.

What Phase 2 eliminates is shared contention *across domains*: a `fileMu` or `statusMu` write no longer blocks any of the five refreshers (`networkStatsSnapshot`, `peerHealthSnapshot`, `peersExchangeSnapshot`, `cmSlotsSnapshot`, `routingSnapshot`). What Phase 2 does **not** eliminate is the intra-domain dependency above — debugging a `get_peers` staleness spike must still consider IP-state writer traffic, not just peer-domain traffic. That intra-domain coupling is intentional because `BannedIPsFn` / `RemoteBannedFn` are correctness gates on the candidate list; the trade-off is that the 500 ms staleness window on the snapshot absorbs those short blocks without reaching the RPC handler.

Last-online persistence is node-owned rather than bus-owned. A direct peer loss
confirmed by clean remote EOF is timestamped when the session closes and observed after `RemoveDirectPeer`; the mutation returns post-removal reachability under the table lock and the same predicate as `Snapshot.ReachableIdentitiesWithTransit`. A witnessed transit
final-route loss is observed by the snapshot comparison. The serialized `presenceProjection` records whether each previously reachable identity had direct and/or transit sources. Direct teardown removes its source under the same lock that serializes snapshot capture; lifecycle consumes the entire transition only when it actually publishes a confirmed final EOF, while an ambiguous direct close leaves any transit source snapshot-owned. Thus ownership is independent of whether direct and transit losses are captured in one snapshot generation or two, and no cross-goroutine dedup marker is needed. Both paths queue
`trustStore.recordLastOnlineAt` through `Service.goBackground` exactly once and
then publish `identity.presence.changed` as a best-effort notification. The
event carries the observing node identity as `Source`; desktop consumers on a
shared bus filter by it. No filesystem I/O runs under a routing lock, and only
the snapshot-reason route subscriber writes `ReachableIDs`.

#### Cluster-mesh Phase 2 — `routing.Table.mu` ownership of `RouteHealthState`

Phase 2 of the cluster-mesh plan (separate from the Service-mutex
Phase 2 above — names collide by accident; wire-protocol contract
lives in `docs/protocol/route_health.md`, ranking contract in
`docs/routing.md` "Route health and composite scoring") introduces
a new per-`(Identity, Uplink)` quality-signal layer:
`RouteHealthState` (Good→Questionable→Bad→Dead state machine),
per-pair RTT EWMA, and the `CompositeScore` ranking function. The
locking ownership decision is enforced as follows:

- `healthStore` lives **inside `routing.Table`** as a leaf data structure (no own mutex). `t.mu` protects it, exactly the same way it already protects `store` and `flap`. A Lookup-path can therefore read uplinks, RTT, and health under a single `t.mu.RLock()` without cross-mutex coordination — this matters because `sync.RWMutex` in Go gives writer priority (a queued writer blocks all new readers), so a "cross-mutex snapshot" pattern would risk deadlocks under load.
- Writer methods that touch health (`MarkHopAck`, `MarkProbeAck`, `MarkProbeFailure`, `TickHealth` — landing in PR 11.2 / 11.3 / 11.4) acquire `t.mu.Lock()` in W mode. They must not perform any blocking I/O (socket write, disk write, peer-callback) while holding the lock — the canonical protocol is "take t.mu, mutate, release, then optionally call `sendFrameViaNetwork[Sync]` outside the lock".
- The `fetchRouteHealth` AND `fetchRouteLookup` RPC handlers (in `internal/core/rpc/routing_commands.go`) call `RoutingProvider.HealthSnapshot()` synchronously per request. That call lands in `routing.Table.HealthSnapshot`, which takes `t.mu.RLock()`, deep-copies every `RouteHealthState` via `healthStore.snapshotLocked`, releases the lock, and returns the value slice. `fetchRouteLookup` joined this path when `Snapshot.Health` was narrowed to the `{Dead ∪ cooled}` routing-relevant subset (its CompositeScore ranking needs the full per-pair tiers); it gates the call on the target having at least one live route, so a lookup miss takes no health lock. Each such RPC invocation therefore briefly grabs `t.mu` in R mode — there is currently NO `atomic.Pointer[healthSnapshot]` cache between RPC and storage. The lock window is one full walk + deep copy of the health store — O(total tracked `(Identity, Uplink)` pairs), which scales with the whole routing table (hundreds of thousands of pairs on a dense node, NOT bounded by the direct-peer count). It runs under R mode so it does not block other readers, but `fetchRouteLookup` now rides this same path on a hit, so a high rate of these diagnostics is real copy/lock pressure (`fetchRouteLookup` gates on a live route to skip misses). Writer paths that need an exclusive lock (`MarkHopAck`, `MarkProbeAck`, `TickHealth`, `UpdateRoute`'s health bookkeeping) compete with concurrent `fetchRouteHealth` calls as they would with any other reader, but Go's `sync.RWMutex` writer-preference still applies so a queued writer blocks new RPC reads.
- Eviction in storage drives health cleanup, at the removal chokepoints. `routeStore.onClaimDropped` (wired to `healthStore.evictUplinkLocked` in `NewTable`) fires under the same `t.mu.Lock()` for every claim that physically leaves storage: in-place cap displacement in `AdmitNew` / `AdmitDirect` and TTL compaction in `CompactExpired`. There is no separate reconcile pass — the previous one ran only when `CompactExpired` removed something, so a cap replacement whose winner kept refreshing its TTL leaked one health entry per displaced uplink indefinitely (28 510 health entries against 3 585 claims on a five-day relay). `RemoveDirectPeer` and `InvalidateTransitRoutes` additionally evict eagerly on tombstoning. `WithdrawRoute` / `InvalidateUplinkClaim` do NOT: the withdrawn claim becomes a tombstone (`Hops=HopsInfinity`, `Withdrawn=true`) and its health entry is dropped when `CompactExpired` removes the tombstone. `Lookup` filters withdrawn claims before health is consulted, so a health entry behind a tombstone never influences ranking, and it is bounded 1:1 by the tombstone it shadows. The invariant is `health keys ⊆ storage keys`; `Table.Usage` reports the health-container length without auditing every pair under `t.mu.RLock`; the full `health keys ⊆ storage keys` audit is test-only. The omitted `route_health_orphans` series is not a measured zero. There is no separate health-only TTL.
- Probe and query send paths (PR 11.3 / 11.4) prepare their target lists under `t.mu.RLock()` (snapshot of Questionable pairs / target identity), release the lock, then iterate the snapshot calling `sendFrameViaNetwork`. Holding `t.mu` across the wire send would couple routing-state critical sections to socket I/O latency — explicitly forbidden.

#### Cluster-mesh Phase 3 — `routing.Table.mu` ownership of reputation fields

Phase 3 of the cluster-mesh plan (see `docs/cluster-mesh/phase-3-multipath-reputation.md` for the full working document, ranking contract update in `docs/routing.md` "Route health and composite scoring") extends the per-`(Identity, Uplink)` quality-signal layer with hop-ack reputation: `HopAckAttempts`, `HopAckSuccesses`, `ReliabilityScore` (EWMA of binary outcomes), `ConsecutiveFailures`, and `CooldownUntil` (black-hole arm). The decision to keep these fields under the same `routing.Table.t.mu` as the Phase 2 `RouteHealthState` is documented in §2.5 of the Phase 3 working document and enforced as follows:

- The reputation fields are **embedded directly in `RouteHealthState`**, not stored in a parallel `reputationStore` with its own mutex. The Lookup path reads `(uplinks, health, RTT, reputation)` atomically under one `t.mu.RLock()`; a cross-mutex coordination would have re-introduced the writer-priority pitfall already navigated by Phase 2 (a queued writer on a sibling mutex would have blocked Lookup readers, defeating the bounded-latency goal).
- Writer methods that touch reputation (PR 12.2 will land `Table.MarkHopAck` extension and the new `Table.MarkHopFailure`; PR 12.4 hooks `applyCooldownExpiryLocked` into `Table.TickHealth`) acquire `t.mu.Lock()` in W mode. Like Phase 2, they must not perform any blocking I/O while holding the lock — multi-path failover (PR 12.3) snapshots the ranked uplink set under `t.mu.RLock()`, releases the lock, then calls `sendFrameToAddressVia` / `sendRelayMessage` outside the critical section.
- The `fetchRouteReputation` RPC handler (PR 12.7, in `internal/core/rpc/routing_commands.go`) mirrors `fetchRouteHealth`: `t.mu.RLock()` + deep copy through `Table.ReputationSnapshot`, no `atomic.Pointer` cache. The cost stays O(N) where N is the number of tracked pairs (≤24 per identity in densely-connected meshes); promotion to a snapshot pointer is deferred until field telemetry shows contention.
- Eviction follows the Phase 2 contract verbatim: reputation fields live inside `RouteHealthState`, so the same `evictUplinkLocked` that drops a health entry also drops its reputation counters. Reputation is not persisted across restart (Phase 3 §4.9 decision #6) — process wipe = cold-start warmup for every uplink.

### Migration strategy

The split is implemented domain-by-domain, starting from the least-coupled domain. Each step is a self-contained commit:

1. `fileMu` — smallest surface, easiest to verify. **[done]**
2. `ipStateMu` — isolated from delivery paths. **[done]**
3. `knowledgeMu` — writes only during handshake. **[done]**
4. `gossipMu` — covers the `fetch_messages` reader path, cleanly bounded. **[done]**
5. `deliveryMu` — covers the relay / outbound queues. **[done]**
6. `peerMu` — largest domain; folded `statusMu` into the same commit. **[done]**
7. `statusMu` — folded in with `peerMu` (aggregate status is derived from peer + delivery state). **[done]**

Phase 2 is complete. The legacy `s.mu` field no longer exists on `Service`; every `*Locked` helper's doc-comment names exactly which domain mutex (or mutexes, in canonical order) the caller must hold. A reader must not assume any prior-era default; consult the helper's doc-comment.

### Testing contract

Every migration step must keep the existing node test suite green. Targeted regression tests from Phase 1 (reader-starvation reproducers in `*_starvation_test.go`) are re-run after every step to confirm the no-starvation property is preserved or improved.

## Address aging ownership

`Service.peerAdmissionMu` is an independent admission mutex: take it before domain locks; hold it across automatic insertion/eviction and the subsequent PeerProvider side effects. It also serializes explicit source bookkeeping in `recordAddedPeer`; the subsequent `registerAddedPeerCandidate` provider update runs after that admission hold is released. No caller takes it while holding a domain mutex. `peerEntry` owns aging counters/timestamps, runtime `ActiveDials` leases and one-shot `AllowImmediateDial`, all under `peerMu`; no parallel address-history map exists. `Service.peerClock` is immutable after construction (tests replace it before concurrent use). Provider recheck round/cursor belong to `PeerProvider.mu`; candidate selection takes its write lock. Provider callbacks may take `peerMu`, so admission releases `peerMu` before provider Remove/Add.

`beginAddressDial` takes `peerMu` and returns a once-only lease release; a failed dial releases it, a successful dial transfers it to `peerSession.releaseAddressDial` before handing the session to CM. That function is immutable afterwards and owns its `sync.Once`: `runCMSession` defers it, and stale-generation disposal also invokes it, outside domain locks. An in-flight primary remains ineligible for eviction through fallback and setup, including before session publication. `noteAddressSessionLocked` requires `peerMu` write and runs before health-alias resolution, only on admissible outbound publication.

Admission chooses/removes the victim and inserts the newcomer in one `peerMu` section. It captures any identity losing its last address anchor before deletion. After unlocking: `ipStateMu` cleans observed-IP history/orphaned votes, then a separate `deliveryMu` section drops pending frames (`statusMu` inside only to refresh counts); provider changes and dirty notification follow with no domain mutex held. Pins/bindings/downgrade marks in the security store are untouched. Periodic `evictStalePeers` is inert; orphaned inbound/socket cleanup retains its separate lifetime.


## Outbound handshake readiness

`peerSession.readiness` is an immutable pointer to a session-local barrier. Its context, wake channel and wait bound are set before the reader starts; state/failure/channel closure are owned by the barrier's independent leaf mutex. Authentication publication takes `peerMu` then this leaf mutex, writes `authOK`, then commits ready. Barrier methods never acquire domain mutexes or call Service. Pending can become ready, aborted or closed; ready survives later dial-context cancellation and wait timeout, while an actual session Close is terminal even after ready. An abort before readiness prevents a late waiter from reactivating the session.

The legacy reader snapshots `admission.awaitsReply("auth_ok")` before inbox delivery, releases that admission mutex, delivers the expected response, then awaits readiness before its next line read/admission. It holds no domain/admission mutex while waiting. Cancellation, Close and the existing `peerRequestTimeout` bound unblock it with a meaningful local error and close the session; no per-frame queue or extra goroutine exists. Unrequested auth_ok and true pre-auth data do not open the gate. Proven v2 starts its reader after intro validation and metadata/auth publication. CM's dial watcher uses the same terminal readiness decision, so cancel after a successful handoff cannot randomly close the ready session.


### Identity pin preview ownership

`secureSessionStore.identities` owns both the original `pinnedAt` and a runtime `evidenceRevision` in each `identityPinEntry`. The store's monotonic proof counter and optional preview HMAC key are owned by its leaf `mu`; there is no parallel identity or token map. Every accepted proof changes only its identity's revision, including a proof of an existing pin. Recreating an entry uses a fresh counter value. Revision exhaustion refuses proof publication locally without wrapping, charging the address or changing existing pins, bindings or v2 marks. Neither revisions nor the process key are persisted; the format-2 file retains identity timestamps and value-only class metadata, and a restart invalidates every prior preview. Metadata shares the same entry/mu; reproof preserves it. Confirmation also commits to canonical metadata, so changing it invalidates a preview without changing proof revision. The independent fence backup retains the full entry; recovery never guesses lost metadata. See [pin metadata/recovery](design/session-v2-pin-metadata.md). The separate monotonic runtime protection revision is owned by the same entry/mu. New entries get fresh protection revisions; reproof preserves them. The private metadata CAS uses `pinMutationGate.RLock → mu`, increments the dirty generation, and performs no I/O or `writeMu` acquisition. Its result certifies only memory staging; the normal writer can persist it later. Both revisions are bound into preview HMAC, so metadata ABA stays stale. Raw metadata assignment is limited to unpublished copies. See [the CAS durability boundary](design/session-v2-pin-metadata-cas.md).

Proof publication and its existing save take `pinMutationGate` for read before entering `update`/`persist`. The order is `pinMutationGate → writeMu → mu`; `mu` still never spans I/O and the gate never spans the network handshake. Preview takes `mu` only after acquiring entropy outside it. Confirmation remains unavailable until verified durable recovery and an external restart fence are implemented and admitted; public confirmation remains unavailable while the internal transaction is verified.


### Desktop identity pin dialog

`consoleModal.identityPinRemoval` and all of its widgets, preview, generation, acknowledgement and pending-request pointer belong solely to the window UI goroutine. Each request captures an immutable identity/token/generation and uses `Window.beginUIOp`/`endUIOp` plus the window cancellation context. The worker owns only its fresh capacity-one reply channel and calls the existing typed in-process command; it never writes dialog state. The UI polls only the current channel and checks generation/identity. Edit, close, reopen or shutdown cancels and removes the old request and credential, so a late reply cannot re-enable confirmation. Diagnostic formatting and JSON serialization of credential-bearing UI state are refused/redacted. No token, parallel identity map, persisted UI history or generic console dispatch is introduced. Exact canonical consequence validation precedes its reviewed translation; acknowledgement and confirmation are separate UI gestures. Public confirmation remains unavailable. See [desktop behavior](operations/identity-pin-desktop.md).

### Identity pin durable transaction and offline recovery

The internal removal port is separate from admission: a fake fence or backend never enables public confirm. Exclusive `pinMutationGate` spans token/revision recheck, external Begin, guard/main/cleanup, receipt and memory publication. Order: `pinMutationGate → writeMu → mu`; `mu` never spans I/O. Begin errors permit no local write. After Begin, a separate 30-second context prevents client cancellation from abandoning cleanup; rollback attempts are bounded.

The guard is bounded to 8192 bytes. Main snapshots preserve endpoints and add enrolled store identity plus the last 32 audit phases (`removal_written`, `protection_restored`); these describe file contents, not an external success acknowledgement. Runtime pin deletion follows durable main, durable cleanup and acknowledged receipt. Cleanup/receipt failure triggers protective rollback. Unconfirmed rollback retains the pin, latches fatal `RecoveryRequired` and stops networking after locks release. The last `Service.Run` return defer preserves fatal error after teardown/final flush; cancellation cannot turn it into exit success.

Scope, audit, recovery latch, `pinMainLoaded` and startup/offline flags belong to store `mu`; scope/audit snapshots are copied. Ordinary proof/flush never clears the latch. `written` advances only through covered changes; concurrent endpoint changes and renewals remain dirty. An absent pin named by the pending permit reserves one global slot until finalized clean restart; actual pin count remains separate. Reproof of that identity consumes its own reservation, while a new different identity sees effective occupancy. Construction checks the guard before sharing the store. A guard or enrolled main without verified supervised admission refuses startup before listeners.

`RecoverIdentityPinProtection` restores protection only and irreversibly bars this Service from Run. A complete original main must have been loaded: missing/corrupt main is never synthesised from one permit, even if the marker disappeared. Recovery preserves every pin/endpoint and accepts an absent guard with an exact external permit, while refusing damaged/foreign guards. Its receipt grants no restart permission. Public removal remains unavailable; the dedicated desktop preview dialog respects the same admission. A concrete verified-session adapter and offline CLI exist as an explicit review candidate; see [candidate lifecycle](operations/identity-pin-recovery-candidate.md).

`secretfile.Dir.DurableWrite`/`DurableRemove` stay relative to the pinned directory and return `NotCommitted`, `Committed` or `Indeterminate`. Directory-sync error after replacement/unlink is unknown. Unsupported backends refuse before mutation; the Windows strict backend remains unavailable. Existing ordinary secret writes retain their prior implementation.

The 30-second storage context limits admission to subsequent stages; it cannot interrupt a kernel-blocked open, rename or fsync. Tests cover returning errors and uncertain acknowledgements, not permanently uninterruptible storage I/O.

---

### Selected-neighbour pin promotion

`Service.selectedPins` is immutable after construction; its one `goRunLoop` worker joins `runLoopsWg`. The capacity-one wake channel coalesces requests. Its independent `passMu` serializes worker passes and owns retry pacing; callers hold no Service/store mutex while acquiring it. A pass snapshots the bounded existing sessions under `peerMu.RLock`, releases it, then consults the pin owner and performs I/O. There is no peer/store cross-lock or extra per-identity work queue.

`peerSession.selectedProof` (opaque proof publication's owner/identity/incarnation binding, actual source group, monotonic proof-ready mark) is immutable before reader start. `selectedGeneration` belongs to `peerMu` and is assigned only after CM accepts its generation. Atomic `eligibilityEnded` becomes terminal at Close or immediately on terminal reader error, before delayed publication cleanup. The live threshold is witnessed before admitting an immutable historical fact; close after that witness may preserve the earned fact. Neither pending age nor an old entry binding crosses connection replacement or pin recreation.

Store `mu` owns entry incarnation, immutable/COW metadata plus runtime strict-ACK revision, and the derived Selected allocation/confirmed counters. All production entry publications/deletions update those counters in the same section; they are not a second live identity owner. Incarnation uses a fresh existing protection-sequence value at creation and remains stable across reproof/metadata changes. Class admission takes `pinMutationGate.RLock → mu`, atomically checks the 4096 allocation bound including an absent C3 backup reservation, then stages through the owner CAS. Unsupported strict-backend preflight precedes new staging. Strict confirmation uses `pinMutationGate.RLock → writeMu → mu`, captures the full snapshot/generation/revisions, releases `mu` for file sync/replacement/parent sync, then acknowledges only unchanged captured protection revisions. No Service/store mutex spans filesystem I/O. Ordinary persistence never grants ACK; unknown outcomes retain counted unconfirmed metadata. See [the promotion contract](design/session-v2-selected-pin-promotion.md).

---

### Connection-owned announce receive evidence

`peerSession.receive` and its outbound `connEntry.receive` share one immutable
pointer created before the first setup read. Every registered inbound connection
owns a fresh `AnnounceReceiveState`. An inbound dispatcher resolves that owner
from the actual ConnID under `peerMu.RLock`; snapshots never own receive evidence.
Close/unregister closes the owner. The state mutex is a leaf: it calls no service,
I/O, registry, or routing-table code while locked. `peerMu → receive.mu` on
unregister has no reverse edge. Handlers release the state mutex before table
updates. A new v3 epoch clears receive-baseline validity under the same mutex as
the epoch update; unchanged-epoch deltas cannot restore it. Identity-level
reconnect/grace hooks still reset send cursors and preserve existing accounting,
but cannot reset or grant connection-owned receive evidence. See
[receive baseline](protocol/receive_baseline.md).

### Explicit local-contact K1 ownership

`trustStore.contacts` remains the sole contact/fact owner. The typed local remember captures a store/identity/runtime-row marker stamp under the original trustMutationMu -> trust.mu apply section before unlock/events; the immutable nonzero-sized marker is JSON-ignored, generic refresh preserves it, delete/recreate/restart reset it. First-fact preflight remains outside trustMutationMu; stage rechecks the exact original stamp under trustMutationMu -> saveMu -> trust.mu before creating authority. Optional fact state is immutable/COW in its row; fact revision, runtime ACK, one proof candidate and the permanent authorityObserved latch belong to trust.mu. The strict writer and writer-strength decision share saveMu; staging first authority takes saveMu then mu, preventing a queued weaker writer from replacing a later ACK. Full snapshot encoding/I/O runs outside mu. Loaded authority is audited/read through a pinned handle before it can be reused, not blessed by a later save. No weak fallback is allowed once loaded/staged authority has been observed.

The contact producer is immutable after Service construction; one joined worker owns its pass, with a capacity-one wake channel. Inventory is bounded by current pin-owner identities (at most20,000), copied under pin mu and released before contact lookups under trust.mu. Only proof-bearing target fact stamps are ACKed; full contact-file writing itself still costs O(contact-book size). Eligibility takes trustMutationMu, briefly reads fact state under trust.mu, releases it, then takes pinMutationGate.RLock -> pin mu for entry-bound CAS. No trust leaf/peer mutex spans pin I/O. It releases trustMutationMu before independent strict metadata confirmation, never recursively acquiring the read gate while an exclusive remover waits. Cancellation is checked after acquired locks; captured proof incarnation and local-intent generation are rechecked after I/O. See [the complete K1 boundary](design/session-v2-local-contact-pin.md).

### Identity lookup action/attempt ownership (R0)

The resolver owns its runtime action generation, exact resolution/attempt rows,
queue state and ephemeral completion claim under `identityResolver.mu`.
Nonserialized stamps are on existing intent rows; mutation/snapshot order is
`resolver.mu → identityIntentStore.mu`. Neither is held during intent disk save,
record verification/import, datagram enqueue, logging or ebus publication. The
existing `saveMu` serializes disk snapshots after those locks; savedGen rejects
out-of-order older snapshots. Registration, queue return, successful completion,
expiry and cancellation recheck the exact owner/generation. Success may preserve
only one existing claimed attempt pending queue admission; the private reducer
returns a once-only scalar soft value to a debug observer after unlocking, then
discards it. No history/worker/pin consumer is added. The owning resolver context
is separate from Window request admission; shutdown revokes runtime authority
without interpreting resumed format1 reasons as trusted actions. Record import
may normally improve a replacement's global key availability, but cannot finish
or charge that replacement's resolution. See
[lookup provenance](design/session-v2-relation-provenance.md).

### Datagram constructor facts

`datagramStartup` belongs outside the seven domain mutexes: NewService writes it once, capturing requested configuration before the instance-owned constructor and recording its actual outcome. It is immutable thereafter; cfg's existing failure-time opt-out still controls advertisement. `DatagramRuntimeState` reads immutable identity/start/observation and the existing atomic plane pointer, with no lifecycle mutable fields, domain lock, queue or I/O. No Ready flag or serving guarantee is introduced. Caller/member ownership/readiness and receipt binding remain separate gates. The private constructor dependency is fixed per construction, never a global mutable test hook.

### Inactive record-only R foundation (R1a)

The existing pin owner/mu also owns the destructive-removal epoch and raw Relation
allocation/confirmed tallies. Put/delete/strict-ACK/load/recovery maintain them
in the same section. All non-absent R allocations count, including Contact+R,
unconfirmed metadata and an absent C3 backup; Selected accounting is unchanged.
The private evidence counter orders session, historical and inactive verified
record protection. External fence `ProofRevision` remains the compatible legacy
wire name, not a TLS proof. Format2 has no new runtime evidence/ACK field.

Observation capture uses `pinMutationGate.RLock -> mu`, with no I/O or resolver
consumer. Existing observations bind store/incarnation; absent observations bind
store/removalEpoch. Removal increments that checked epoch under exclusive gate
and mu immediately before external BeginRemoval after prior preflight/guard/token
checks, and retains it after every error, Unknown or protective restore. No owner
mutex spans BeginRemoval. No per-identity tombstone/history is introduced.

The unused private verified-record stage takes `pinMutationGate.RLock -> mu`,
checks owner/capacity/raw-R/reservation/revisions before atomic publication and
performs no I/O. There is no production constructor/consumer/queue/rate worker.
A separate record publication capability rechecks current store/incarnation and
revisions before/after the shared close/drain/purge barrier outside all domain,
store and removal locks. Cancellation preserves the existing owed-purge handoff.
It never manufactures ProvenIdentity or republishes a pin from a stale ticket.
See [the exact inactive scope](design/session-v2-relation-pins.md).

### SDK/process final accounting ownership

SDK lifecycle owns the final snapshot after all normal managed transport/background joins; readers use an atomic pointer to an immutable value and receive copies. A process has one wait owner and one constant-size coalescing signal watcher. Signal admission holds terminalMu through the syscall; public Stop uses a short request lock and preserves context/confirmation bounds. Reaped excludes signals; the waiter joins the watcher and publishes copied exit facts before Done. A short publication/request fence preserves earlier StopUnconfirmed evidence. Post-Done reduction uses a separate context-aware gate; no terminal/member lock spans retained-root I/O. Cancellation retains ownership and returned observations never alias sealed storage. See [the exact boundary](design/loadstand-final-accounting.md).

### Inactive contact signed-record protection (K2A)

`trustStore.pinEvidenceMu` is a scalar RW gate, not another record/fact inventory. WRITE joins
common merge/own-record memory replacement, contact creation/promotion/forget (including early
exits), and bound local-fact creation. Order is optional trustMutationMu/saveMu → evidence WRITE →
trust.mu; both evidence/trust locks are released before persistence. Fact ACK, proof-pointer and
last-online refresh preserve fact ID/revision and do not need this gate. Unpublished load owns its
bytes. Inputs and recordFor outputs clone raw signed Body/Sig plus parsed DTypes; recordBodies
clones DTypes. recordLocked/session getLocked borrow owner values under trust.mu only.

Inactive K2 capture/stage uses candidate.mu → trustMutationMu → pinMutationGate.R → evidence.R →
brief trust.mu, released before brief pin-store.mu. Evidence.R spans current persistent record
comparison through pin stage. Already-locked pin helpers avoid recursive RLock behind a waiting C3
writer. Crypto/preflight occur outside these owner locks. Existing snapshotLocked still clones the
contact/conflict maps and base64-encodes all persistent records inside trust.mu/evidence sections;
that preserved proportional cost is neither O(1) nor encoding-free.

The caller-owned owed mutex serializes one explicit completion attempt, with no reverse edge or
new live queue. Fresh strict coverage uses removalGate.R → writeMu → brief store.mu capture/ACK;
JSON/backend work is outside store.mu. Exact captured revisions alone receive current ACK; the
receipt is historical K coverage, not confirmation of concurrent newer metadata. All trust/store/
evidence/removal locks are released before actual common legacy close/drain/purge. Current contact
or old evidence revision does not revoke earned cleanup; exact incarnation/earned K/store safety
are checked before/after. Record ingress remains inactive; the existing joined contact worker owns bounded completion
retention. See [K2A boundary](design/session-v2-contact-record-pin.md).

### Inactive R2a own-message / first-hop fixture boundary

Ordinary admission/CM/reseed carry nil; only private unused fixture roots opt in.
Preparation captures original X under the removal read gate and short store mu,
before message-store I/O. After storage releases admission/gossip locks, the
optional early entry registration uses `pinMutationGate.RLock → deliveryMu →
store.mu` and the locked observation helper: no recursive RWMutex acquisition,
I/O, signature verification, route or event under these locks. Body-digest
checks in `prepared.matches` / `r2aCurrentLocked` still hash Payload while the
owner locks are held: O(payload), part of the relative per-owner cost rather
than constant-time validation. It rechecks the original owner rather than
recapturing X, and skips later normal registration for that branch.

Actual route/exact-session/capability snapshot uses peerMu alone, released before
the same gate→delivery→store capture section. The common first directed builder
owns the fresh ACK on its one item. All locks are released before offer/sendMu;
the writer only closes the witness channel, with no callback into owner locks.
Settlement uses gate→delivery→store to check that retained ACK/current row and
original X/Y bindings once. Removal/cancellation serializes through existing
delivery ownership; missing/terminal rows cannot be reconstructed. No strict
writer or live R-stage edge is added. See [inactive outgoing provenance](design/session-v2-outgoing-provenance.md).

### K2 owned completion and cancelled drain waiters — English

The optional classified pin metadata owns at most one record ticket and a store.mu-owned progress
mirror. COW/ACK preserves both; removing/changing earned Contact, including zero metadata, refuses
before revision effects. Stage builds the prospective binding/ticket before publishing the entry.
A present already-K1/loaded K without a ticket creates no synthetic record job/barrier. Exact
binding + same ticket + earned Contact validates final mirror publication after the real barrier;
generic ACK alone cannot complete it. Store selectors/status never take owed.mu.

The existing contact worker owns its scalar RR cursor/clock and one selected reference. Selection
is O(<=20k pins) under store.mu, without a retained batch or second map. K1 runs before each K2
opportunity; Busy uses TryLock. Whole-pass start spacing and cooperative K2 deadline are 2 seconds;
wakes remain coalesced during pacing. A blocked syscall/lock still owns its worker/reference and
shutdown cannot claim join until it returns. External handles/C3 copies/full snapshots cost more
than the20k resident + one selected-reference inventory; no resource acceptance is implied.

unprovenRouting.mu is a leaf. Waiter removal may run while the invoking completion holds owed.mu;
the permitted order is owed.mu -> unprovenRouting.mu, with no reverse edge. Registration, removal,
leave and owePurge take only the routing-writer mutex, never a ticket/store/trust/evidence/removal/
write lock, and invoke no callbacks. The caller holds no store/trust/evidence/removal/write lock
during registration/removal/drain waiting. The routing-writer mutex is released before waiting.
Cancelled wait removes only its own channel, nils the removed slice tail and never closes it or
changes owed/inflight. Final leave closes survivors only after last-writer purge; a leave race makes
removal a safe no-op. One worker therefore retains at most one active drain subscription.


### Inactive FindNode option-A core ownership

`intakeCoordinator.mu → pending.mu → ONE ForwardCalendar.mu → target Session.state.mu` is the
composed mutation order. Pending/calendar locks may be held together; no lock below Session is
acquired after its ID peek. The uninterrupted ID lock covers final validation and infallible commit.
Standalone PendingTable still takes pending→Session, standalone calendar only itself, allocator only
Session. No reverse edge, public-wrapper recursion, callback, crypto or I/O is introduced.
Classification executes before entry. Composed snapshots use the same order after mutation locks
are released. Only the private owner can access its subowners; no production caller is installed.
Group records/cursors/registry belong to outer.mu. Row phases require outer+pending; bucket/aliases
require outer+calendar. Dispatch completion retains the SAME row until actual Finish. Cancellation
never refunds calendar debt. See [the inactive core contract](design/find-node-option-a-core.md).

### Inactive inbound quota journal (N0)

`secureSessionStore.mu` owns format discrimination, logical clock/domain/content generation, covered/strict stamps, lazy bounded row arena and derived raw Inbound tally. Its immutable elapsed leaf is SinceBoot, with no wall fallback. Legacy format2 never attaches/samples a journal. Format3 loads attach validated complete state with a fresh runtime anchor; no production installer or mature-witness producer exists. Test-only installation uses mutationGate.W → writeMu, releases mu for namespace preflight/encoding/I/O, and publishes only under mu.

Private paired admission orders mutationGate.R → fixture lifetime leaf → brief mu. The terminal owner takes only that leaf and never the gate/store lock. Preflight is outside both leaves; context and original incarnation are rechecked after waits. Strict writers order mutationGate.R → writeMu → brief mu; C3 uses W → writeMu → mu. They never acquire the lifetime leaf. No trust/peer/domain lock, signature verification or filesystem/external I/O spans mu. Bounded row expiry/insertion/copy work does span mu (up to4096×520 bytes/262144 ticks), with no constant-latency claim. Encoding/hash occurs after release. Writers capture a fixed journal stamp before I/O; retaining the full DTO for post-write ACK would unnecessarily retain its large maps. Captured content/tick ACK cannot clear newer phase/progress or complete K2's owned drain/purge obligations. Existing periodic/stop owners checkpoint observed time with both no-op predicates progress-aware; no new timer/queue/worker is introduced. N1 lifecycle wiring and resource admission remain separate.

### Inactive R1b record admission owner

The normal identity resolver keeps a nil recordAdmission pointer. A private
fixture-only factory owns one fixed256-slot relationRecordAdmission under the
existing resolver.mu; the observed clock/backend opener are immutable after
construction. No extra owner mutex/map/queue is added. Prospective, answer-await,
qualified, Processing and earned work share the same physical capacity. Existing
attempt/resolution optional refs are non-owning companions; no caller target map
can rebind original evidence. Record-protection reconcile snapshots slot-held
refs with resolutions under resolver.mu, then releases it before other work.

Registration uses pinGate.TryRLock -> resolver.mu -> store.mu.TryLock and releases
all before SendLocal. Final transaction order is pinGate.R -> evidence.R ->
resolver.mu -> trust.mu.R -> store.mu, released in reverse. Clock samples are
bounded local leaves. The shared sampler strips wall monotonic readings with
Round(0) before retaining startup/original/current/createdAt; the independent
elapsed value remains the only rate/startup authority. No crypto, disk/network I/O or legacy drain occurs under
these locks. Existing resolver -> intents/recordProtection leaf edges remain;
store/trust/recordProtection never acquire resolver in reverse. Trust R stays
held through K classification/stage, including durable contact confirmation.

A single separately joined worker performs synchronous preflight/strict I/O and
unlocked legacy close/drain/purge, with whole-pass2s pacing and cooperative2s
attempt context. The context is not a hard syscall bound. Processing storage is
not cleared/reused until actual worker return; shutdown may wait. Strict coverage
holds pinGate.R -> writeMu, briefly store.mu for snapshot, releases store.mu for
encoding/I/O, then briefly ACKs only captured revisions and pre-I/O N-in stamp.
The gate is released before the barrier. Reproof/unrelated COW preserves the
earned incarnation/allocation obligation; C3 recreation makes it stale. After
actual worker join one StoppedOwed head may remain in the same closed slot,
without raw/action/resolution/seq-floor replay authority. No default worker,
activation, across-restart owed replay or resource acceptance is asserted.

### Inactive original inbound lifetime ownership (N1)

Ordinary Service construction leaves `inboundPins` nil. The private unused factory publishes that immutable pointer only after valid format3 load and strict-backend preflight, before sharing the Service. Its one run-loop owner joins producer exit; `passMu` serializes one synchronous attempt, cursor and current selection. A cap1 wake never bypasses the relative retry deadline. Actual runtime wrappers pass nil into shared runWithFiles/attempt; only a private fixture lends the existing typed strict-files port until synchronous join. No backend is retained in the producer. Real Run cancellation/join and common scheduler timing are separate test oracles. No per-session worker/timer, new registry or outgoing-queue field is added.

`peerMu → original lifetime.mu` protects exact R/T/W/entry/ConnID binding, capture and publication. Publication reads the SAME immutable N0 elapsed leaf AFTER both mutex waits and final auth validation, then commits ready/Published without another wait. This is only the trusted nonreentrant memory/clock leaf exception: no arbitrary callback, signature verification, network/filesystem I/O or logging is allowed there. Selection releases peerMu before paired admission or strict work. End holds lifetime.mu only, clears authority chains and never calls back to Service/store, logs, does I/O or joins another owner.

Paired admission remains `pinMutationGate.R → lifetime.mu → store.mu`; strict confirmation uses `pinMutationGate.R → writeMu → store.mu`; C3 uses W→writeMu→store.mu. Strict never takes lifetime.mu. End-first forbids allocation; stage-first retains its paired content and strict obligation. Pending alone is not owed earned metadata. No store→peer/lifetime reverse edge is introduced; the producer releases store.mu before registry selection. Snapshot encoding and strict I/O occur outside peer/lifetime/store mutexes.

Original raw and decoded wrappers share one optional leaf, fixed before exposure. Read/Write errors and Close End before delegated/diagnostic work. The handler's End defer runs BEFORE heartbeat join. All owned bodies use the captured handle; incoming context stays on the synchronous dispatch→pipeline→push stack. Existing background initial-push/heartbeat closures capture that original leaf rather than a fresh ID lookup. Terminal aliases retain no proof/socket/store/entry chain. Existing jobs are a separate J term, not proved bounded by live B sessions.

Bound SEND captures core+original leaf in one peerMu section. Eviction Ends its captured SEND owner before diagnostics; actual Close separately selects and Ends its current target under peerMu, releases peerMu, then closes the captured core. Immutable send/close selection values retain respectively core+original handle / core only, with no Service, ConnID or callback for completion-time relookup. Their shallow costs are two pointers / one pointer, in addition to existing concurrent callers; this is not a bound on the number of callers. Stale collection uses its existing single temporary slice and Ends the actual selected owners before warnings; loopback condemnation releases its own leaf before End while peerMu still fixes selection. Push repeat-rate warning runs after limiter unlock and original End. No logging or signature verification is newly moved under lifetime/store locks; bounded N0 expiry/copy work retains its documented cost. Cooperative timeouts do not forcibly interrupt kernel-blocked I/O.

### Inactive F1 proof-transport foundation

The private, unused FindNode factory composes a memory-only transport adapter. Ordinary constructors leave it nil and still advertise neither overlay capability. It installs no handler, admission, class provider, worker, signer or socket writer. Explicit-cap genuine TLS fixtures do not change production hello/welcome capabilities. A canonical `SessionSeed` captures actual local/remote negotiated facts immediately after Accept/Dial; copies share one consumption latch. Invalid Bind arguments do not consume it. The seed and Session retain identity proof values, not a full TLS ProvenSession or connection. End closes the one canonical Session without refund or I/O.

One `proofTransportLifetime.mu` orders original terminal, accepted/CM publication and the independent N-in companion. Raw R and decoded W, the registry and the outbound peer alias the SAME cell. An atomic provisional terminal latch plus bind-once CAS covers Read/Write/Close before the cell is attached; it cannot mint proof. End clears proof/socket/entry/peer/store chains before existing blocking diagnostics. Accepted and dialled datagrams capture an original terminal-only arrival capsule before the synchronous pipeline; outbound initial push captures its original owner before background scheduling. No current-ID/address lookup substitutes another transport.

Accepted publication follows actual auth. Outbound publication follows the CM generation-accepted callback, successful setup/registration and the legacy-pin recheck, immediately before SessionInitReady. It samples independent monotonic elapsed time AFTER owner-lock waits. The coverage is only **registered accepted and CM post-setup readers**; it excludes one-shot/unregistered readers and does not claim complete overlay admission or already-consumed CM Active. CM deactivation/stale-success refusal Ends the selected original session before later teardown diagnostics.

FindNode observes registry keys through `forEachConnIDLocked` and resolves each with `connEntryByIDLocked` while the same `peerMu.RLock` remains held. The ID-only walker includes registered nil-core entries and exposes no core or retained inventory. Each original lifetime check still takes `common.mu` under `peerMu`; cancellation returns no partial snapshot. CM publication uses the same point-lookup facade under its unchanged `peerMu` → `common.mu` critical section.

N-in remains listener-only, with the same original mandatory pin/incarnation, ten-minute continuous lifetime, source-group journal/debit, strict ACK and owed-work semantics. A dialler has no N-in companion. An N-in clock failure cannot mint its class or suppress a separately valid F1 publication. No limits, activation, resource admission or release gates change. One cell/optional companion per original transport and an on-demand O(current registered readers) snapshot are structural costs; TLS evidence before publication, snapshot allocations, external delayed handles and full process/heap costs remain additional.

## Russian

### Неактивный K stage исходного push

Классы admission immutable; только неиспользуемый private Service candidate выбирает K. Reservation
держит resolver.mu -> kStageMu, physical256 включает Processing. Stage: candidate.mu -> trustMutationMu
-> pinMutationGate.R -> pinEvidenceMu.R -> краткий trust.mu.R (освобождён) -> store.mu -> kStageMu.
Clock и exact Backup.Restore выполняются ДО leaf при непрерывных store/removal/evidence locks;
entry/permit сверяются по полным значениям/указателям без decode/hash. Под leaf только bounded values,
COW/tallies, публикация owed и стандартный внутренний context check после последнего ожидания.
Нет leaf -> ранних locks/IO/clock/crypto/Restore/notification. Nil foundation разделяет memory body.
Shutdown resolver.mu -> leaf отзывает pending, оставляет Processing, отпускает locks, затем вызывает
фиксированные cancel functions. Только actual callback return освобождает slot; connWg/runLoopsWg
доказывают transport join. K close по LIFO до inbound drain. Borrowed typed файлы принадлежат только
fixture, их Preflight вне locks; production всегда nil. Отмена и успешный mock Preflight не join/ACK.


### Диагностика runtime и банов (T5 A4/R1)

`RuntimeMetrics` читает runtime процесса без Service locks. `banDiagnosticsSnap` — неизменяемый
`atomic.Pointer`-снимок, `banDiagnosticsAccessNanos` ограничивает периодическую работу активностью
читателя; они синхронизируют себя вне семи доменов. Канал `banDiagnosticsRequests` создаётся в
`NewService`, содержит не больше одного ожидающего запроса; worker принадлежит
`hotReadsRefreshLoop` и завершается вместе с ним. Неизменяемая эпоха `banDiagnosticsLifecycle` (context и канал остановки) публикуется атомарным
указателем: до старта/после остановки чтение недоступно, отмена worker прерывает очередь/ожидание
даже при заблокированной сборке. Worker после сборки и читатель перед возвратом перепроверяют
эпоху. Свежий RPC не захватывает доменных мьютексов. Worker сначала заканчивает callbacks PeerProvider, затем отдельно
копирует баны под `peerMu`, отдельно IP score/баны под `ipStateMu`, отпускает их и читает атомарные
счётчики loopback-наказаний. Сортировка и JSON вне locks. Источники имеют явное окно наблюдения,
это не новая общая транзакция. Кешированное чтение свежесть не обещает.
[Контракт диагностики](rpc/runtime_metrics.md).

### Мотивация

`node.Service` исторически защищал всё in-memory состояние единым `sync.RWMutex` (`s.mu`). Этот мьютекс покрывал как минимум семь разных доменов — peer-подключения, очереди доставки сообщений, криптографическое «знание», gossip-топики, IP-политики, файловый трансфер и агрегированный статус. `sync.RWMutex` в Go отдаёт приоритет писателям: один поставленный в очередь writer блокирует всех новых readers до тех пор, пока не завершатся уже активные readers. Любая запись в любой домен сериализовала чтения во всех остальных.

Под штормом переподключений это давало класс багов reader-starvation: `fetch_network_stats`, `fetch_peer_health`, `get_peers` и другие read-mostly RPC стояли 10–15 с пока дренировалась очередь peer-management writers.

Фаза 1 решила проблему горячих read-путей через atomic snapshots вне `s.mu`. Фаза 2 атакует корень: сам мьютекс. Поля `Service` естественно раскладываются по семи независимым доменам — запись в один домен не должна блокировать чтения в другом.

### Карта доменных мьютексов

Каждое поле, ранее защищённое `s.mu`, переезжает ровно в один из мьютексов ниже. Все мьютексы — `sync.RWMutex`; read-dominated домены выигрывают от split RWMutex, и единообразная форма упрощает ревью.

Таблица полей — см. английскую секцию выше.

**Штрафные записи ключуются `penaltySubject`, а не названной identity.** `peerQuarantine`, `peerDisconnectHistory`, `peerAnnounceHistory` и `lastResyncAccepted` (все под `peerMu`), корзины `announceLimiter` и записи hop в `nonDMKeySync` ключуются `penaltySubject` (`penalty_subject.go`): identity — только если её доказала v2-сессия на этом соединении, иначе — адрес, который набрал этот узел, TCP-IP источника или само соединение для loopback. Вычисление субъекта не добавляет рёбер блокировок. Для исходящей сессии это `peerSession.penaltySubject()` по полям, записанным в литерале структуры (`address`, `proven`) и больше не переписываемым, — блокировка не нужна. Для принятого соединения — `penaltySubjectOfCore`, который читает только собственное синхронизированное состояние NetCore (`Auth()`, `RemoteAddr()`); поэтому `trackInboundDisconnectWithPresenceEvidence` вычисляет его из уже полученного core, не входя в `peerMu` повторно. `connPenaltySubject` берёт `peerMu.RLock` через `netCoreForID` и вызывается только диспетчером приёма, вне всех доменных мьютексов. Путь закрытия получает субъект закрывающейся сессии параметром (`onPeerSessionClosedWithAttribution`), поэтому `maybeArmRouteQuarantineOnCloseLocked` начисляет его в той же секции `peerMu`, что и раньше. Транзитный гейт (`isPeerTransitQuarantinedLocked`) читает только запись доказанной identity под той же блокировкой, что и раньше. См. `docs/protocol/network_security.md` §13.

**Legacy-заявки identity, которой нужен v2** (`v2_identity_claims.go`). `identityRequiresV2` читает pin-хранилище под его листовым `mu`, а затем отдельно берёт `peerMu.RLock`, чтобы найти живое v2-соединение (`identityHasProvenConnection`); вместе они не удерживаются никогда, а вызывающий не держит доменных мьютексов — вызовы идут из обработчиков приёма, `refuseLegacyIdentity` (hello, welcome, повторная проверка в `auth_session` и исходящая повторная проверка сразу после регистрации legacy-сессии), `ReactionsSupportOf` (до того, как он сам берёт `peerMu.RLock`) и путей hop-ack. `onIdentityProvenV2` выполняется в `openInboundTransport` / `provenTransport` после записи pin и ДО регистрации v2-соединения, без доменных мьютексов: `closeLegacyClaimsOf` снимает снапшот legacy-соединений с этой identity под `peerMu.RLock`, освобождает его и только потом закрывает их (закрытие проходит через собственный читатель соединения, который берёт `peerMu`); затем `forgetClaimedAddressesOf` забывает адреса, которые с этой identity связывает только заявка: снимок адресов, привязанных к identity, берётся из хранилища (`endpointsBoundTo`, только листовой `mu` хранилища) ДО взятия `peerMu`, заявленные записи `peerIDs` и identity их `persistedMeta` удаляются за одно удержание `peerMu.Lock`, затем он освобождается, и только после этого peers.json помечается к следующей записи (`markPeerStateDirty`, который сам берёт `peerMu`). Проход — O(N) по `peerIDs` и `persistedMeta` за одно удержание `peerMu.Lock`, один раз на каждое доказательство v2, того же порядка стоимости, что и чистки выселения по тем же картам. Каждая запись identity адреса идёт через `recordAddressIdentityLocked`, который под `peerMu.Lock` писателя отклоняет заявку identity с сохранённым pin, читая листовой `mu` pin-хранилища под `peerMu` (`peerMu → mu хранилища`; под этим `mu` не вызывается ничего, кроме доверенных часов, и не берётся ни один мьютекс, кроме листового `clock.mu` часов — см. абзац о хранилище ниже, — поэтому ребро цикла не замыкает). Pin сохраняется до того, как чистка берёт `peerMu`, поэтому заявка, записанная до её удержания, ею забывается, а попытка после — отклоняется: путь v1, чья проверка pin прошла до доказательства, заявку не вернёт; затем `unprovenRouting.waitDrained` ждёт — не держа никаких мьютексов, — пока каждый legacy-обработчик маршрутного входа этой identity, допущенный до pin, не закончит запись; и только после этого `forgetUnprovenRoutingResidue` вызывает `routing.Table.ForgetUplink` (только `t.mu`) и реестр анонсов, не удерживая доменных мьютексов. Обработчик допускается через `admitRoutingInput`, который учитывает его (`enter`) ДО повторного чтения `identityRequiresV2`: либо обработчик видит pin и отказывается, либо доказательство видит учтённый обработчик и ждёт его — окна «проверка прошла, запись легла после очистки» нет. Учтённый обработчик не держит доменных мьютексов, а его запись берёт только собственный лок таблицы маршрутов, поэтому ожидание цикла не замыкает. Если контекст вызывающего закончился раньше (таймаут dial, а не только остановка узла), очистка не теряется: `owePurge` передаёт её последнему выполняющемуся обработчику, чей `release` (`releaseRoutingInput`) вызывает `forgetUnprovenRoutingResidue`, оставаясь учтённым как выполняющийся, — поэтому доказательство, ждущее в это время, просыпается после неё, — а отменённое доказательство завершается ошибкой, и его соединение не регистрируется. Поэтому `release` вызывается без доменных мьютексов: каждый обработчик отпускает допуск после записи и вне своих локов, а колбэк таймера hop-ack выполняется после освобождения `rs.mu`. `unprovenRouting` (`unprovenRoutingWriters`) владеет листовым мьютексом вне доменной схемы — см. ниже.

Вне схемы остаются:

- `listener` — пишется один раз в `Run` до `Accept`. Существующая дисциплина «один writer» сохраняется.
- `done`, `connWg`, `backgroundWg`, `peerActivityNanos`, `trafficMu` / `lastTrafficSnap`, `*Snap` atomic pointers — уже имеют собственную синхронизацию (chan, WaitGroup, `sync.Mutex`, `atomic.Pointer`). Не входят в доменное разделение.
- `backgroundMu` (`sync.Mutex`) / `backgroundClosed` (`service.go`) — ВОРОТА ДОПУСКА перед `backgroundWg`. Вне семидоменного разделения, с собственной синхронизацией: `backgroundMu` — leaf, охраняет только `backgroundClosed` и упорядочивает `backgroundWg.Add`, который `goBackground` делает под ним; никакой другой мьютекс под ним не берётся, через I/O и callback он не удерживается, поэтому рёбер в канонический порядок не добавляет. `goBackground` проверяет флаг и делает `Add(1)` в одной критической секции и сообщает, принята ли задача; `Run` взводит флаг в САМОМ ПЕРВОМ зарегистрированном defer, то есть он выполняется ПОСЛЕДНИМ — после дренажа входящих соединений, после того как `stopRunLifecycle` дождался всех циклов жизненного цикла и горутин исходящих сессий, после остановки подсистем и `close(s.done)`. Работа, которую ставит сам teardown, поэтому ещё принимается и дожидается `WaitBackground`; работа, запрошенная после возврата из `Run`, отклоняется и не стартует. Именно это не даёт `WaitBackground` — его вызывают после `Run`, прямо перед закрытием хранилищ и удалением каталога данных — гоняться с `Add`, поднимающим счётчик с нуля (неправильное использование `WaitGroup`, о котором сообщает race detector), и не пускает квитанции, дозвоны key-sync и записи trust store на остановленный узел. `WaitBackground` ворота НЕ закрывает: unit-тесты используют его как барьер на Service, у которого `Run` не вызывался, и после него продолжают ставить работу. Отказ логируется на debug с местом вызова; три вызывающих, которые что-то держат под задачу, его обрабатывают: `runSenderKeySyncPass` сам возвращает in-flight слот (`release`), `triggerRouteQueryAsync` освобождает in-flight слот цели (`releaseInFlight`), `tryEnqueueGossipJob` возвращает `gossipPoolShutdownDrop`. Остальные — fire-and-forget на окончательно остановленном узле: отказ для них равен выходу процесса. **Для продолжений исключения нет:** задача, принятая до возврата из `Run` и запросившая продолжение уже после, — `pushBacklogToSubscriber` → `goBackground(confirmEnvelopesDurably)`, `writePushFrame` → `confirmEnvelopeOnWire` → долговременное подтверждение в `goBackground`, — отклоняется как любая другая. Её последствия такие же, как у краша в этот момент (конверт остаётся неподтверждённым на диске), и восстанавливается она так же, как после краша: ретраями доставки на стороне отправителя и долговременными журналами при следующем старте. Лог отказа: `background_job_refused_after_run_returned` на debug, с полями `node`, `op` (`goBackgroundOp` называет операцию; `goBackground` пишет `unnamed`) и `caller`. Закреплено `TestBackgroundJobIsRefusedOnceRunHasReturned`, `TestBackgroundJobIsAdmittedWhenRunWasNeverCalled`, `TestARefusedSenderKeySyncPassReturnsItsSlotExactlyOnce`, `TestTheGossipFallbackDropsAJobOnceRunHasReturned`, `TestARefusedRouteQueryReleasesItsInFlightSlot` и `TestRunServiceForTestDrainReportsUnfinishedBackgroundAsError`. У `WaitBackground` есть предусловие: `Run` не выполняется — после возврата из `Run` ни одна задача не может быть принята, а на Service, у которого `Run` не вызывался, вызывающий сам владеет каждой горутиной, ставящей работу.
- **`Run` не присваивает ни одного поля, которое может прочитать вызывающий Service.** Приложение запускает RPC-сервер и сборщик метрик ДО вызова `Run` (`internal/app/node/app.go`), а desktop- и SDK-рантаймы отдают Service интерфейсу, пока `Run` стартует в собственной горутине, поэтому любой обработчик локальных фреймов и любой метод RPC-провайдера может выполняться одновременно со стартом `Run`. Обычное поле, присвоенное там, публикуется этим читателям без ребра happens-before — это гонка данных, а не деталь порядка старта, и ожидание `connManager.Ready()` лишь прячет её от тех, кто знает, что ждать надо. Значение, нужное таким читателям, строится в `NewService`; значение, которое может построить только `Run`, публикуется атомарной записью, которую читатели загружают первой. Поля под этим правилом:
  - `runCtx` / `runCancel` — контекст жизненного цикла Service и его отмена; создаются в `NewService` (`context.WithCancel(context.Background())`) и после этого иммутабельны; `context.Context` безопасен для конкурентного использования, поэтому чтение идёт без мьютекса. `Run` его не заменяет: он заставляет его СЛЕДОВАТЬ за контекстом вызывающего (`context.AfterFunc(ctx, runCancel)`) и отменяет его на любом выходе, причём страхующий `defer` регистрируется до первого `return`. Два следствия — намеренные: работа, захватившая `runCtx` ДО `Run` (например, горутина восстановления sender-key), всё равно завершается демонтажом `Run`, тогда как прежнее присваивание в `Run` оставляло её на контексте, который никто бы не отменил; дедлайн и значения контекста вызывающего не переносятся — переносится только отмена. `Run` одноразовый (закрывает `done`), так что отменённый `runCtx` после возврата `Run` — окончательное состояние. Закреплено тестами `TestTheLifecycleContextTakenBeforeRunEndsWithRun` и `TestReadingTheLifecycleContextDuringRunStartupIsNotADataRace`.
  - `captureManager` — строится в `NewService` (`newCaptureManager`, привязан к `runCtx`), после этого иммутабелен и синхронизирован собственным мьютексом `capture.Manager`, поэтому читатели (RPC захвата трафика, `peerHealthFrames` за `fetch_peer_health`, тапы соединений) не берут доменных мьютексов. `Run` владеет только его остановкой (`defer s.captureManager.Close()`). Он существует до `Run`, так что RPC захвата, обслуженный до `Run`, попадает в тот же менеджер, а не получает отказ; `nil` остаётся только в Service-литералах тестов, поэтому проверки на nil сохранены. Закреплено тестами `TestTheCaptureManagerExistsBeforeRun` и `TestCaptureRPCDuringRunStartupIsNotADataRace`.
  - `gossipJobs` / `gossipNoticeJobs` — единственное исключение, которое по-прежнему присваивается в `Run` (`startGossipDispatch`), потому что тесты пула запускают его напрямую на Service-литералах. Каналы ПУБЛИКУЮТСЯ записью `gossipPoolUp.Store(true)`, идущей после присваивания, и диспетчер читает канал только после того, как `gossipPoolUp.Load()` вернул true: `tryEnqueueGossipJob` принимает АДРЕС поля и разыменовывает его за этой загрузкой. Чтение поля в месте вызова — прежняя форма — гонялось с `Run` даже у тех, кто ждёт `connManager.Ready()`, потому что пул стартует после него. Закреплено тестом `TestGossipDispatchDuringRunStartupIsNotADataRace`.
  - Присваивания в `Run`, которые остаются безопасными, и почему: `listener` (под `peerMu`), `relayStates.onHopAckTimeout` (пишется до `relayStates.start()`, чей оператор `go` и есть ребро к единственному читателю — TTL-тикеру), дескрипторы файл-трансфера (под `fileMu`). Новое присваивание в `Run` обязано назвать здесь своё ребро в том же коммите.
- `unattributedNonDMDrops` — счётчик `atomic.Uint64` не-DM-пушей, отвергнутых из-за неизвестного ключа автора (`refuseUnattributedNonDM`). Собственная синхронизация, только наблюдаемость.
- `nonDMKeySyncPasses`, `nonDMKeySyncSkipped` — счётчики `atomic.Uint64` бюджета синхронизации ключей для не-DM (`nondm_key_sync.go`). Собственная синхронизация, только наблюдаемость.
- `trustedKeysAtLoad` — что `NewService` сделал с ключами trust store (`classifyContactKeys`: отвергнутые контакты, отброшенные box-пары). Записывается один раз в `NewService`, затем иммутабелен; lock не нужен.
- `relayLimiter`, `announceLimiter`, `connLimiter`, `cmdLimiter` — четыре rate-лимитера. Каждый указатель присваивается один раз в `NewService` и больше не заменяется, поэтому его чтение не берёт доменный мьютекс. Устойчивость к nil определяется местом вызова, а не полем: все вызовы `announceLimiter` и два ИСХОДЯЩИХ хелпера `cmdLimiter` (`outboundControlFrameAllowed`, `dropOutboundControlFrameBucket`) проверяют nil, потому что Service-литералы в тестах оставляют эти лимитеры равными nil; входящий командный путь (`admitInboundCommandLine`, defer с `removeConn` в `handleConn`), relay fan-out (`relayViaGossip`, `tryRelayToCapableFullNodes`), очистка `sweepIdleRateLimiters` в `bootstrapLoop` (она вызывает `relayLimiter.sweepIdle`, `connLimiter.sweepIdle` и `cmdLimiter.sweepIdle` без проверки; её вызов `announceLimiter.sweepIdle` проверяет nil) и `connLimiter.allowConnect` в accept-цикле `Run` разыменовывают указатель без проверки и требуют non-nil лимитер. Каждый лимитер владеет собственным листовым `sync.Mutex`: relay-, announce- и command-лимитеры — через общий `tokenBucketSet` (`token_bucket.go`), лимитер соединений — напрямую.
